How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.

Full answer
-----------

The 2025 and 2026 U.S. western Atlantic bluefin tuna allocations (before any carryover/underharvest adjustments) are shown in Document 6, Table 5:

- 2025
- Baseline quota: 1,316.14 mt
- Plus longline bycatch set‑aside: 25 mt
- Total U.S. allocation = 1,341.14 mt
- 2026 (under ICCAT Recommendation 25‑05)
- Baseline quota: 1,509.98 mt
- Plus longline bycatch set‑aside: 62.5 mt
- Total U.S. allocation = 1,572.48 mt

Difference: 1,572.48 − 1,341.14 = 231.34 mt

So, 231.34 metric tons more went to the United States for 2026 than for 2025, including the tonnage reserved for the longline bycatch fishery.

Reasoning
---------

We need answer: "Before under- or overharvest adjustments, how many more metric tons of western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States for 2026 than for 2025, whatever fishery the tonnage was reserved for (to two decimals)?"

Need understand TAC? Actually they ask western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States for 2026 than for 2025, before under- or overharvest adjustments, whatever fishery the tonnage was reserved for.

We need determine U.S. baseline quota for 2025 and 2026, including the pelagic longline bycatch set-aside? They said "whatever fishery the tonnage was reserved for" meaning include all U.S. allocations including bycatch set-aside? Need parse documents precisely.

Let's review documents for bluefin tuna quotas.

Document 3 (Proposed rule, Jan 5? Actually Document 3 is proposed rule on quotas published May 7, 2026? Let's check details.

Document 3: Federal Register Volume 91 Issue 88 (Thursday, May 7, 2026) Proposed Rules "Atlantic Highly Migratory Species; North Atlantic Swordfish, South Atlantic Swordfish, North Atlantic Albacore, and Atlantic Bluefin Tuna Quotas"

In Document 3, they discuss implementing recent ICCAT recommendations including bluefin tuna. They propose to implement increased U.S. baseline quota adopted by ICCAT in 2025, divide among domestic subquota categories, and implement changes to bluefin tuna quota associated with longline bycatch adopted by ICCAT in 2025.

Specifically in Document 3 summary: "For bluefin tuna, this action also proposes to implement the increased U.S. baseline quota adopted by ICCAT in 2025, divide it among the established regulatory domestic subquota categories, and implement changes to the bluefin tuna quota associated with longline bycatch adopted by ICCAT in 2025."

Then in Document 3 supplementary information they discuss baseline quota. Let's find numbers.

In Document 3, under "Bluefin Tuna Annual Quota, Subquotas, and Adjustment Process" they discuss current and proposed.

They say: "In 2025, ICCAT adopted Recommendation 25-05, which increased the bluefin tuna TAC to 3,081.6 mt and increased the U.S. quota to 1,509.98 mt for 2026-2028. The current U.S. percentages ... If the overall TAC is greater than 2,660 mt, as is currently the case, the U.S. receives 49 percent of that TAC. Recommendation 25-05 further increased the U.S. allocation for longline bycatch of bluefin tuna from 25 mt to 62.5 mt. The Recommendation states that the 62.5 mt allocation is for bycatch related to longline fisheries... Recommendation 25-05 removed a provision from a prior recommendation that provided for subtracting the longline bycatch allocation from the TAC before calculating the individual country quotas. That section defines the Gulf of America region as all waters of the U.S. exclusive economic zone west and north of the boundary stipulated at Sec. 600.105(c) and the Atlantic region as all other waters of the Atlantic Ocean including the Northeast Distant gear restricted area (NED), a large area whose coordinates are set forth in Sec. 635.2.

As described above, this rulemaking would increase the pelagic longline bycatch set-aside quota from 25 mt to 62.5 mt consistent with ICCAT Recommendation 25-05. In recognition of new information regarding increased mixing between the western Atlantic and eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna stocks in the western Atlantic bluefin tuna management area, Recommendation 25-05 modifies the area where the set-aside applies for longline fisheries from ``in the vicinity of the management area boundary'' as specified in Recommendation 22-10 (and previous iterations of western Atlantic bluefin tuna management measures) to ``in the vicinity of the management area boundary and adjacent areas.'' Additionally, Recommendation 25-05 now specifies that the set-aside comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC as opposed to the western Atlantic TAC."

Ah, this is key! The bycatch set-aside (25 mt in 2025, 62.5 mt in 2026) comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC, not the western Atlantic TAC.

The question specifically asks about "western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC".

So for the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC allocation to the US:
- 2025: 1,316.14 mt (baseline quota)
- 2026: 1,509.98 mt (baseline quota)

The difference would be 1,509.98 - 1,316.14 = 193.84 mt

Reasoning
---------

I need to find the difference in the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC (Total Allowable Catch) allocated to the United States between 2026 and 2025, before any under- or overharvest adjustments.

Let me search through the documents for information about bluefin tuna quotas for 2025 and 2026.

From Document 3 (Federal Register, Volume 91 Issue 88, Thursday, May 7, 2026), I find this relevant information:

"In 2025, ICCAT adopted Recommendation 25-05, which increased the bluefin tuna TAC to 3,081.6 mt and increased the U.S. quota to 1,509.98 mt for 2026-2028. The current U.S. percentages of the bluefin tuna TAC were first established in Recommendation 10-03. If the overall TAC is greater than 2,660 mt, as is currently the case, the U.S. receives 49 percent of that TAC. Recommendation 25-05 further increased the U.S. allocation for longline bycatch of bluefin tuna from 25 mt to 62.5 mt. The Recommendation states that the 62.5 mt allocation is for bycatch related to longline fisheries in the vicinity of the management area boundary and adjacent areas and that this allocation is derived from the eastern Atlantic and Mediterranean bluefin tuna TAC. Full answer

Finding the Answer

The question asks for the increase in western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC allocated to the U.S. between 2025 and 2026, before under/overharvest adjustments, counting all fishery categories.

2025 U.S. allocation from the Western Atlantic TAC:

From Document 6 (Table 5 and accompanying text), the 2025 baseline U.S. quota was 1,316.14 mt, plus a 25 mt pelagic longline bycatch set-aside — for a total of 1,341.14 mt.

Critically, the documents confirm the 25 mt set-aside was drawn from the western Atlantic TAC: "Recommendation 25-05 now specifies that the set-aside comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC as opposed to the western Atlantic TAC." The old provision subtracted the 25 mt from the WA TAC before calculating country quotas, and that 25 mt went to the U.S.

→ 2025 WA TAC to U.S. = 1,341.14 mt

2026 U.S. allocation from the Western Atlantic TAC:

Recommendation 25-05 set the WA TAC at 3,081.6 mt, of which the U.S. receives 49% = 1,509.98 mt (the new baseline). The 62.5 mt bycatch set-aside is derived from the eastern Atlantic and Mediterranean TAC (not the WA TAC).

→ 2026 WA TAC to U.S. = 1,509.98 mt

Difference:

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.14 = \boxed{168.84 \text{ mt}}$$

Calculation

From Document 5 (Table 5), the bluefin tuna quotas before under-/overharvest adjustments (i.e., total quota = baseline + bycatch set-aside) are:

| | 2025 | 2026 | |---|---|---| | Baseline Quota | 1,316.14 mt | 1,509.98 mt | | Bycatch set-aside | 25.0 mt | 62.5 mt | | Total Quota | 1,341.14 mt | 1,572.48 mt |

The difference:

$$1{,}572.48 - 1{,}341.14 = 231.34 \text{ mt}$$

231.34 metric tons more of western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States for 2026 than for 2025 (before any under-/overharvest carryover adjustments).

2025 baseline quota: 1,316.14 mt
2025 total quota (baseline + bycatch allocation): 1,341.14 mt (with 25 mt set-aside)

2026 baseline quota: 1,509.98 mt
2026 total quota (baseline + bycatch allocation): 1,572.48 mt (with 62.5 mt set-aside)

"Before under- or overharvest adjustments" means we look at the total quota (baseline + bycatch set-aside) before carryover adjustments.

2025: 1,341.14 mt
2026: 1,572.48 mt

Difference: 1,572.48 - 1,341.14 = 231.34 mt

So 2026 total = 1,572.48 mt
2025 total = 1,341.14 mt

Difference = 231.34 mt

- 2026 Total Quota: 1,572.48

The answer is 231.34 metric tons.

Calculating the Increase in Western Atlantic Bluefin Tuna TAC to the U.S. (2025 vs. 2026)

Key Information from Document 3

For 2026 (Recommendation 25-05):

- Western Atlantic TAC = 3,081.6 mt
- U.S. share (49%) = 1,509.98 mt (the codified baseline, all from western Atlantic TAC)
- The 62.5 mt pelagic longline bycatch set-aside is derived from the eastern Atlantic TAC — not western

For 2025 (previous recommendation):

- Under the old provision, 25 mt was subtracted from the western TAC before calculating country quotas, then returned to the U.S. as a bycatch set-aside (also from western TAC)
- U.S. baseline = 49% × (TAC_old − 25), plus 25 mt set-aside
- From the current subquotas (Alternative C1): General = 710.7 mt = 54% × baseline → baseline = 710.7 ÷ 0.54 = 1,316.11 mt
- Total western Atlantic TAC to U.S. = 1,316.11 + 25 = 1,341.11 mt

Calculation

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.11 = 168.87$$

Full answer
-----------

Setting Up the Comparison

2026 U.S. allocation (Document 3):

ICCAT Recommendation 25‑05 raised the U.S. quota to 1,509.98 mt for 2026–2028 (49% of the 3,081.6 mt TAC), and increased the pelagic longline bycatch set‑aside (reserved for longline fishermen) from 25 mt to 62.5 mt. Because the old provision of subtracting the bycatch allocation from the TAC before calculating country quotas was removed, the document states the total directly:

> "the overall U.S. quota under Recommendation 25‑05 is 1,572.48 mt, of which 62.5 mt can be used only by pelagic longline fishermen."

So: 1,509.98 + 62.5 = 1,572.48 mt (before any under/overharvest carryover).

2025 U.S. allocation:

The current (2025) baseline quota is not stated outright, but it can be recovered from the "no action" (Alternative C1) subquota figures, which are generated by applying the fixed codified percentages (Amendment 13) to the baseline quota:

| Category | Current subquota | % of baseline | Implied baseline | |---|---|---|---| | General | 710.7 | 54% | 1,316.1 | | Harpoon | 59.2 | 4.5% | ~1,315.6 | | Longline | 209.3 | 15.9% | ~1,316.4 | | Trap | 1.3 | 0.1% | (rounding) |

710.7 ÷ 0.54 = 1,316.1 mt (consistent with the other categories and with the sum 980.5 ÷ 0.745 = 1,316.1).

The current pelagic longline bycatch set‑aside is 25 mt (listed separately in the C1 analysis, and stated as the amount "increased ... from 25 mt to 62.5 mt"). The proposed regulation's structure ("baseline ... not including an additional ... bycatch set‑aside") confirms this set‑aside is in addition to the baseline.

So 2025 total = 1,316.1 + 25 = 1,341.1 mt.

Difference

1,572.48 − 1,341.1 = 231.38

(In terms of components: the baseline quota rose by 1,509.98 − 1,316.1 = 193.88 mt, and the longline bycatch set‑aside rose by 62.5 − 25 = 37.5 mt; 193.88 + 37.5 = 231.38.)

Final answer: 231.38 metric tons

Full answer
-----------

The 2025 and 2026 U.S. baseline (pre-adjustment) bluefin tuna quotas are shown in the final rule (Document 6, Table 5):

- 2025 Baseline Quota: 1,316.14 mt ww
- 2026 Baseline Quota: 1,509.98 mt ww

(The 2026 increase reflects ICCAT Recommendation 25-05, which raised the western Atlantic TAC so that the U.S. 49% share equals 1,509.98 mt; Document 3 and Document 6.)

Difference: 1,509.98 − 1,316.14 = 193.84 mt

So, before any under/overharvest adjustments, 193.84 metric tons more of the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States in 2026 than in 2025.

Recommendation 25-05 further increased the U.S. allocation for longline bycatch of bluefin tuna from 25 mt to 62.5 mt. The Recommendation states that the 62.5 mt allocation is for bycatch related to longline fisheries in the vicinity of the management area boundary and adjacent areas and that this allocation is derived from the eastern Atlantic and Mediterranean bluefin tuna TAC. Recommendation 25-05 removed a provision from a prior recommendation that provided for subtracting the longline bycatch allocation from the TAC before calculating the individual country quotas. As such, the overall U.S. quota under Recommendation 25-05 is 1,572.48 mt, of which 62.5 mt can be used only by pelagic longline fishermen.

Full answer

Analysis

2026 (from the western Atlantic TAC): The U.S. baseline quota is 1,509.98 mt — 49% of the 3,081.6 mt western Atlantic TAC (3,081.6 × 0.49 = 1,509.984 ≈ 1,509.98). The additional 62.5-mt pelagic longline bycatch set-aside is explicitly "derived from the eastern Atlantic and Mediterranean bluefin tuna TAC" (Rec. 25-05), so it is not part of the western Atlantic TAC draw.

2025 (from the western Atlantic TAC): Under the prior recommendation, the 25-mt longline bycatch allocation was "subtract[ed] from the TAC before calculating the individual country quotas" — i.e., it was carved out of the western Atlantic TAC as well. So the U.S. draw from the western Atlantic TAC was:

- Baseline share (49% of the TAC net of the 25-mt set-aside): 1,316.14 mt
- Plus the 25-mt bycatch set-aside (also from the western Atlantic TAC): 25 mt
- Total from the western Atlantic TAC: 1,341.14 mt

(This matches Table 5's "Total Quota (Baseline Quota + Bycatch Allocation)" of 1,341.14 mt for 2025.)

Calculation

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.14 = 168.84 \text{ mt}$$

Answer

168.84 metric tons more of the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States in 2026 than in 2025 (before under- or overharvest adjustments).

Note: the overall U.S. quota (which includes the 62.5-mt set-aside drawn from the eastern Atlantic/Mediterranean TAC) rose by 231.34 mt (1,572.48 − 1,341.14), but only 168.84 mt of that increase reflects additional western Atlantic TAC.

Full answer
-----------

Finding the Difference in Western Atlantic Bluefin Tuna TAC to the U.S.

Identifying the Key Quotas (Before Adjustments)

From Table 5 in Document 6 (the final rule):

| | 2025 | 2026 | |---|---|---| | Baseline Quota (from western Atlantic TAC) | 1,316.14 mt | 1,509.98 mt | | Bycatch set-aside allocation | 25 mt | 62.5 mt | | Total Quota | 1,341.14 mt | 1,572.48 mt |

Determining the Western Atlantic Portion

The critical distinction comes from Recommendation 25-05, which (as stated in Document 3):

> "now specifies that the set-aside comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC as opposed to the western Atlantic TAC"

and

> "removed a provision from a prior recommendation that provided for subtracting the longline bycatch allocation from the TAC before calculating the individual country quotas."

This means:

- 2025: The 25 mt set-aside was derived from the western Atlantic TAC → Total western Atlantic to U.S. = 1,316.14 + 25 = 1,341.14 mt
- 2026: The 62.5 mt set-aside comes from the eastern Atlantic/Mediterranean TAC → Total western Atlantic to U.S. = 1,509.98 mt

Calculation

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.14 = \boxed{168.84 \text{ metric tons}}$$

Reasoning
---------

Full answer
-----------

Calculating the Difference in Western Atlantic Bluefin Tuna TAC to the U.S. (2026 vs. 2025)

Key Data from the Documents

From Table 5 in Document 6 (final rule), the pre-adjustment U.S. bluefin tuna quotas are:

| Component | 2025 (mt) | 2026 (mt) | |---|---|---| | Baseline Quota (49% of western Atlantic TAC) | 1,316.14 | 1,509.98 | | Pelagic longline bycatch set-aside | 25.0 | 62.5 | | Total Quota | 1,341.14 | 1,572.48 |

Critical Distinction: Source of Bycatch Set-Aside

Per Document 3 and Document 6:

- 2025: Under the prior ICCAT recommendation, the 25 mt bycatch set-aside was subtracted from the western Atlantic TAC before calculating country quotas. The U.S. therefore received 49% × (TAC − 25) = 1,316.14 plus the 25 mt set-aside — all from the western Atlantic TAC = 1,341.14 mt.
- 2026: Under Recommendation 25-05, the 62.5 mt bycatch set-aside is derived from the eastern Atlantic/Mediterranean TAC, not the western Atlantic. The U.S. receives 49% of the full western Atlantic TAC = 1,509.98 mt from the western Atlantic.

Calculation

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.14 = 168.84 \text{ mt}$$

Answer

168.84 metric tons more western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States in 2026 than in 2025.

Reasoning
---------

From Document 6 (Table 5):
- 2025 Baseline Quota: 1,316.14 mt
- 2025 Total Quota (Baseline + Bycatch Allocation): 1,341.14 mt (with 25 mt bycatch set-aside)
- 2026 Baseline Quota: 1,509.98 mt
- 2026 Total Quota (Baseline + Bycatch Allocation): 1,572.48 mt (with 62.5 mt bycatch set-aside)

Final answer: ## Final Answer | Your answer (gold): 168.84 mt

Passages quoted:

- Quoted Doc 3: “Recommendation 25-05 now specifies that the set-aside comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC as opposed to the western Atlantic TAC”
- Quoted Doc 3: “the overall U.S. quota under Recommendation 25-05 is 1,572.48 mt, of which 62.5 mt can be used only by pelagic longline fishermen.”
- Quoted Doc 6: “The United States is allocated an additional 25 mt in 2025 and 62.5 mt in 2026 to account for bycatch related to pelagic longline fisheries.”

Named Doc 6, Doc 3

Full answer
-----------

Step 1 – Find the U.S. allocation from the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC for each year (before any under/overharvest carryover)

From Document 6 (final rule, Table 5 and preamble) and Document 3 (proposed rule):

2025 (Recommendation 10‑03 / pre‑25‑05 framework):

- The prior recommendation provided for subtracting the longline bycatch set‑aside from the TAC before calculating individual country quotas, and it came from the western Atlantic TAC ("Recommendation 25‑05 now specifies that the set‑aside comes from the eastern Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC as opposed to the western Atlantic TAC").
- U.S. baseline quota (49% of the TAC remaining after the carve‑out): 1,316.14 mt ww
- U.S. longline bycatch set‑aside, carved out of the western TAC: 25.00 mt ww
- Total U.S. from the western Atlantic TAC, 2025 = 1,316.14 + 25.00 = 1,341.14 mt ww

(matches Table 5's "Total Quota (Baseline + Bycatch Allocation), 2025 = 1,341.14")

2026 (Recommendation 25‑05):

- New western Atlantic TAC = 3,081.6 mt; U.S. share = 49% → 1,509.98 mt ww (this is the entire U.S. draw on the western Atlantic TAC)
- The new 62.5 mt set‑aside is expressly derived from the eastern Atlantic and Mediterranean bluefin tuna TAC — so it does not come out of the western Atlantic TAC.
- Total U.S. from the western Atlantic TAC, 2026 = 1,509.98 mt ww

Step 2 – Subtract

$$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}341.14 = 168.84 \text{ mt ww}$$

(Note: the total U.S. allocation, including the east‑Atlantic‑derived set‑aside, rose from 1,341.14 to 1,572.48 mt — a difference of 231.34 mt — but only 1,509.98 mt of the 2026 allocation is drawn on the western Atlantic TAC. These are also pre‑adjustment figures, i.e., before the 74.8 mt underharvest carryover and the quota transfers, which the question excludes.)

Final Answer

168.84 metric tons (whole weight) more western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC went to the United States in 2026 than in 2025.

The 2026 baseline quota reflects ICCAT Recommendation 25-05, which increased the bluefin tuna TAC to 3,081.6 mt and the U.S. share to 1,509.98 mt (49% of the TAC, since the TAC exceeds 2,660 mt).

Difference: $$1{,}509.98 - 1{,}316.14 = \boxed{193.84 \text{ metric tons}}$$

The United States was allocated 193.84 mt more of the western Atlantic bluefin tuna TAC for 2026 than for 2025, before any underharvest or overharvest adjustment.
